{"body":"## Motivation\n\nThe content library is currently built with Claude Code **Workflow** fan-out (`work/content_bank_build/build_book_draft.workflow.js`): one agent per pericope, each running the prompt-builder, drafting, running the gates, and repairing via multi-turn tool use. This is effective but coupled to the Claude Code harness, burns tokens on exploratory tool turns, and its rate-limit/server-error failures have to be reconciled by hand.\n\n**Key realization:** the pipeline is already ~90% deterministic Python. Everything the agent shells out to is a pure function:\n\n| Agent step | Actual callable |\n|---|---|\n| `build_brief_prompt PID` | `content_bank.author.build_brief_prompt.build(pid, book)` |\n| write the brief | **LLM call** |\n| `build_draft_prompt PID` | `content_bank.author.build_draft_prompt.build(pid, book, brief)` |\n| draft the items | **LLM call** |\n| verify a quote | `content_bank.lib.corpus_bridge.passage_text(range)` |\n| quote / schema gate | `quote_check.py`, `content_bank.lib.schema.validate_item` |\n| fix until clean | **repair LLM call**, fed gate errors |\n| stage | `content_bank.author.store_writer.upsert_items(book, items)` |\n\nThe agent only needs a shell because it is a *generic* agent in a sandbox. A standalone program calls those functions directly and bakes the results into the prompt — so the LLM's whole job reduces to **two templated calls (brief, draft) + a repair loop**, no tools, no agent framework.\n\n## Proposal\n\nAdd a committed standalone builder (e.g. `content_bank/author/build_cli.py`) that:\n\n- Iterates pending pericopes (and optionally sections) from the manifest.\n- Per unit: `build()` the brief prompt → LLM → write brief → `build()` the draft prompt → LLM → parse JSON → run deterministic gates → repair loop (≤N) feeding gate errors back → stage as `review_status: reviewed` (never `published`; honest provenance, no `confirmed_by`).\n- Explicit rate-limit backoff and per-unit failure isolation (`GateError` / `RateLimitError` → log and continue), so no half-written drafts to reconcile.\n- Matches the lean pipeline exactly: no AI self-review, no self-publish. Human review remains the real gate.\n\n### New deterministic gate (recommended)\n\nAdd `refs_in_range`: reject any `ref` / quoted verse whose location falls outside the pericope's own `range`. This would have **mechanically caught the MAT-035 drift** (a Matthew-15 quote inside a Matthew-8 pericope) that only surfaced via the quote gate by luck. Pure function; the agent version does not even have it.\n\n## Critique / tradeoffs\n\n**Gains:** cheaper + faster (~2-3 flat calls vs ~6-12 tool-use turns per pericope); runs anywhere (cron/CI, no harness); scriptable, resumable, unit-testable; you own the failure handling; reproducible-ish with low temperature.\n\n**Losses / risks:**\n1. **No exploratory self-correction** (agent could re-read and fix scope drift). Mitigated — and arguably reversed — by the `refs_in_range` gate.\n2. **Billing model changes** — moves from the Claude Code subscription's session/usage limits to Anthropic **API credits** (separate wallet, real per-token cost, no monthly cap absorbing it).\n3. **Gates only catch mechanical wrongness** (quotes, schema, ref range) — not weak pedagogy or subtly WCF-off framing. That is exactly why items stay `reviewed`, not `published`; the human review gate is the true quality control. Green gates must not lull anyone into skipping it.\n4. **No adversarial review** — but the shipped lean pipeline already dropped it, so this is parity, not regression; re-addable as one more `llm()` call.\n\n**Net:** for a *draft* library a human reviews anyway, the agentic framing is overkill. A standalone program is cheaper, more controllable, more testable, and with the ref-in-range gate catches a class of error the agents missed.\n\n## Open questions\n\n- **Provider / dependency:** the repo is deliberately **Python-stdlib-only** (per CLAUDE.md). Options: (a) add the `anthropic` SDK — first third-party dep; (b) hit the REST endpoint via `urllib` to **preserve the stdlib-only invariant** (recommended).\n- **Scope:** pericope path only, or sections too (`build_section_brief_prompt`)?\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Standalone builder produces the same `reviewed` items the workflow does, gated on quote + schema (+ new ref-in-range) checks.\n- [ ] LLM access encapsulated behind a single `llm()` seam (swappable/mockable).\n- [ ] Rate-limit backoff + per-unit failure isolation.\n- [ ] Unit tests with the LLM seam mocked (no network in tests).\n- [ ] Decision recorded on stdlib-only vs `anthropic` SDK.","labels":[],"state":"OPEN","title":"Standalone Python content-bank builder (replace agent workflow with an API-call program)"}

=== README head ===
# MxLens API Service

Social listening data platform built on FastAPI. Serves a REST API (`/api/v1/`) and an MCP tool server (`/mcp`) for AI agent integration. Heavy work — clustering, LLM annotation, data import — is dispatched to Celery workers across CPU, GPU, and LLM queues. Analytics queries run in-process via DuckDB over Parquet files stored in MinIO. Redis handles brokering, caching, metadata, and pub/sub streaming.

## Quick Start

```bash
# Install dependencies
uv sync --dev

# Start everything (Docker services + API + Celery workers)
./scripts/mxlens-env.sh start

# Check service health
./scripts/mxlens-env.sh status
curl http://localhost:8765/health

# Stop all services
./scripts/mxlens-env.sh stop
```

## Features

- **DataSource Management** — import from files, Apache Doris, or JSON; chunked Parquet storage in MinIO
- **Enrichment Pipelines** — clustering (DBScan/SimHash), LLM annotation, sentiment analysis via Celery
- **Analytics Engine** — named metrics (time series, frequency, ranking, crosstab, etc.) via DuckDB
- **Evidence/Insights** — peak window detection, top contributors, representative sampling
- **MCP Server** (`/mcp`) — 29 tools across 5 groups (datacore, statmetric, enrichment, evidence, task)
- **SSE Streaming** (`/stream/`) — real-time task progress updates
- **Celery Workers** — 3 queues: CPU (import/export), GPU (clustering), LLM (annotation)
- **REST API** (`/api/v1/`) — datasources, enrichment, statistics, insights, tasks

## Documentation

- **API Docs**: http://localhost:8765/docs (requires `x-api-key` header)
- **Usage Guide**: [doc/USAGE.md](doc/USAGE.md)
- **Architecture**: [doc/design/api_service_architecture.md](doc/design/api_service_architecture.md)

## Development

```bash
# Run tests
uv run pytest tests/ -v

# Run tests with coverage
uv run pytest tests/ --cov=mxlens

# Run just the API (without mxlens-env.sh)
uv run uvicorn mxlens.main:app --reload

# Start Docker services separately
docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.allinone.yml up -d

# Individual mxlens-env.sh commands
./scripts/mxlens-env.sh start-docker   # Docker services only
./scripts/mxlens-env.sh start-api      # API server only
./scripts/mxlens-env.sh start-celery   # Celery workers only
```

## License

1	"""Generic LLM batch endpoint.
2	
3	POST /api/v1/llm/batch              → 202 (submit, returns task envelope)
4	POST /api/v1/llm/batch/estimate     → 200 (sync token + cost estimate)
5	
6	Polling and result retrieval use the existing /api/v1/tasks endpoints. This
7	endpoint is deliberately minimal: caller hands in fully-rendered prompts, one
8	LLM call per prompt, raw text comes back. Row-packed batch labeling stays on
9	the `create_datasource → llm_labeling` path.
10	"""

14

16	from mxlens.models.llm_schemas import (
17	    LLMBatchEstimateRequest,
18	    LLMBatchEstimateResponse,
19	    LLMBatchRequest,
20	)
21	from mxlens.services.llm_service import LLMService
22	
23	logger = structlog.get_logger(__name__)
24	
25	router = APIRouter(prefix="/llm", tags=["llm"])
26	
27	
28	@router.post(
29	    "/batch",
30	    status_code=status.HTTP_202_ACCEPTED,
31	    summary="Submit a batch of fully-rendered LLM prompts (async)",
32	)
33	async def submit_llm_batch(
34	    request: LLMBatchRequest,
35	    service: LLMService = Depends(get_llm_service),
36	    client=Depends(get_current_client),
37	):
38	    """Submit prompts to the configured LLM and get a task_id back.
39	
40	    Each prompt runs as one independent LLM call (no row packing). Poll the
41	    returned task via GET /api/v1/tasks/{task_id} and fetch results from
42	    GET /api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/result.
43	
44	    For batched row-packed labeling on structured data, use the existing
45	    `create_datasource → llm_labeling` path instead.
46	    """
47	    return await service.submit_batch(request, client_id=client.client_id)
48	
49	
50	@router.post(
51	    "/batch/estimate",
52	    response_model=LLMBatchEstimateResponse,
53	    summary="Estimate token usage and cost for a prompt batch (sync, no LLM call)",
54	)
55	def estimate_llm_batch(
56	    request: LLMBatchEstimateRequest,
57	    service: LLMService = Depends(get_llm_service),
58	    client=Depends(get_current_client),
59	):
60	    """Pre-flight estimate. Tokenizes each prompt with the model's tokenizer
61	    and multiplies by the model's input/output prices. Output tokens are
62	    assumed (per-request override or server default) — no LLM call is made.
63	    """
64	    return service.estimate(request)

3	Backs POST /api/v1/llm/batch and POST /api/v1/llm/batch/estimate. Submits the

24	logger = structlog.get_logger(__name__)

26	_CACHE_PREFIX = "llm:cmpl:"
27	
28	
29	def _completion_key(model: str, msgs: list, overrides: dict) -> str:
30	    """Stable cache key over the semantics that determine the output:
31	    model + full message list + completion overrides (NOT api_base/api_key)."""
32	    canonical = json.dumps([model, msgs, overrides], sort_keys=True,
33	                           ensure_ascii=False, default=str)
34	    return _CACHE_PREFIX + hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
35	
36	
37	class _CachedAI:
38	    """Minimal stand-in for a batch_llm result, reconstructed from cache."""
39	    __slots__ = ("content", "additional_kwargs")
40	
41	    def __init__(self, content: str, tool_calls):
42	        self.content = content
43	        self.additional_kwargs = {"tool_calls": tool_calls} if tool_calls else {}
44	
45	
46	def _cache_client(cache):
47	    """Resolve the `cache` arg to a redis-like client (or None). True -> the
48	    shared sync Redis; a client with mget/setex -> used as-is (injection/tests)."""
49	    if cache is True:
50	        try:
51	            from mxlens.utils.cache import get_redis
52	            return get_redis()
53	        except Exception as exc:  # Redis down -> degrade to no cache, never fail the call
54	            logger.warning("llm.cache.unavailable", error=str(exc)[:120])
55	            return None
56	    if cache and hasattr(cache, "mget"):
57	        return cache
58	    return None
59	
60	
61	# Pass shape: convert pydantic ChatMessage models to plain dicts before
62	# handing them to Celery (JSON-only serialization). Carries tool-calling
63	# fields through so the worker can forward them to the LLM.
64	def _serialize_prompts(prompts: list) -> list:
65	    out: list = []
66	    for p in prompts:
67	        if isinstance(p, str):
68	            out.append(p)
69	            continue
70	        serialized: list[dict] = []
71	        for m in p:
72	            turn: dict = {"role": m.role}
73	            if m.content is not None:
74	                turn["content"] = m.content
75	            if m.tool_calls:
76	                turn["tool_calls"] = m.tool_calls
77	            if m.tool_call_id:
78	                turn["tool_call_id"] = m.tool_call_id
79	            if m.name:
80	                turn["name"] = m.name
81	            serialized.append(turn)
82	        out.append(serialized)
83	    return out
84	
85	
86	def _resolve_model_or_raise(model: str | None):
87	    """Build the LLM client at submit time so a bad model surfaces as 400."""
88	    from mxapi import chatmodels
89	
90	    resolved = model or settings.llm_default_model
91	    model_obj, errmsg = chatmodels.build_chat(modelId=resolved)
92	    if errmsg or model_obj is None:
93	        raise ValidationError(
94	            f"Unknown LLM model: {resolved}",
95	            details={"resource_type": "llm_model", "resource_id": resolved, "reason": errmsg},
96	        )
97	    return resolved, model_obj
98	
99	
100	class LLMService:
101	    """Submit and estimate generic LLM batches."""
102	
103	    @staticmethod
104	    def run_batch_sync(prompts: list, model: str | None = None,
105	                       config: dict | None = None,
106	                       build_overrides: dict | None = None,
107	                       progress=None, cache=False) -> dict:
108	        """Synchronous LLM batch execution — the shared core of the run_llm_batch
109	        Celery task AND the mxlens-analyst in-recipe seam. OpenAI-format prompts
110	        in, ``{generations, summary}`` out (with per-prompt + total token/cost).
111	
112	        Args:
113	            prompts: list where each item is a str (single user message) or a
114	                list of ``{role, content, tool_calls?, tool_call_id?, name?}``
115	                turns (OpenAI/LiteLLM format; carries tool-calling fields).
116	            model: chatmodels.menu id; defaults to settings.llm_default_model.
117	            config: LiteLLM completion overrides forwarded per call.
118	            build_overrides: kwargs forwarded to ``build_chat`` (e.g.
119	                ``api_base``/``api_key`` for a self-hosted endpoint).
120	            progress: optional ``(fraction: float, step: str) -> None`` callback
121	                (the Celery task passes its ``update_progress``).
122	            cache: completion cache. ``False`` = off; ``True`` = the shared sync
123	                Redis (keyed by model+messages+config, TTL
124	                ``settings.llm_completion_cache_ttl_s``); a redis-like client is
125	                used as-is. Only cache MISSES hit the LLM; cached prompts are
126	                marked ``cached=True`` and contribute 0 to the usage/cost totals.
127	                Degrades to no-cache if Redis is unavailable.
128	        """
129	        from mxapi import chatmodels
130	
131	        prog = progress or (lambda *_a: None)
132	        start = time.time()
133	        resolved_model = model or settings.llm_default_model
134	        overrides = dict(config) if config else {}
135	
136	        prog(0.02, f"Resolving model: {resolved_model}")
137	        model_obj, errmsg = chatmodels.build_chat(modelId=resolved_model, **(build_overrides or {}))
138	        if errmsg or model_obj is None:
139	            raise ValueError(f"Failed to build LLM client for model={resolved_model}: {errmsg}")
140	
141	        def _to_messages(item) -> list:
142	            """str -> [{'user'}]; list -> plain dicts carrying tool-call fields."""
143	            if isinstance(item, str):
144	                return [{"role": "user", "content": item}]
145	            if isinstance(item, list):
146	                out: list[dict] = []
147	                for turn in item:
148	                    msg: dict = {"role": turn.get("role", "user")}
149	                    content = turn.get("content")
150	                    msg["content"] = "" if content is None else content
151	                    if turn.get("tool_calls"):
152	                        msg["tool_calls"] = turn["tool_calls"]
153	                    if turn.get("tool_call_id"):
154	                        msg["tool_call_id"] = turn["tool_call_id"]
155	                    if turn.get("name"):
156	                        msg["name"] = turn["name"]
157	                    out.append(msg)
158	                return out
159	            raise ValueError(f"Unsupported prompt item type: {type(item).__name__}")
160	
161	        prog(0.05, f"Preparing {len(prompts)} prompts...")
162	        msgs_list = [_to_messages(p) for p in prompts]
163	
164	        # Cache lookup (opt-in) — only MISSES are dispatched to the LLM.
165	        n = len(prompts)
166	        rc = _cache_client(cache)
167	        keys = [_completion_key(resolved_model, m, overrides) for m in msgs_list] if rc else [None] * n
168	        hits: list[dict | None] = [None] * n
169	        if rc:
170	            try:
171	                for i, x in enumerate(rc.mget(keys) if keys else []):
172	                    if x:
173	                        hits[i] = json.loads(x.decode() if isinstance(x, (bytes, bytearray)) else x)
174	            except Exception as exc:
175	                logger.warning("llm.cache.read_failed", error=str(exc)[:120])
176	                rc = None
177	
178	        miss_pos = [i for i in range(n) if hits[i] is None]
179	        prog(0.10, f"Dispatching {len(miss_pos)}/{n} calls to {resolved_model}...")
180	        ai_by_pos: list = [None] * n
181	        if miss_pos:
182	            ai_miss = model_obj.batch_llm(msgs=[msgs_list[i] for i in miss_pos], **overrides)
183	            if len(ai_miss) < len(miss_pos):  # batch_llm short-list padding
184	                ai_miss = list(ai_miss) + [
185	                    type(ai_miss[0])("{}") if ai_miss else None
186	                    for _ in range(len(miss_pos) - len(ai_miss))
187	                ]
188	            for local_i, pos in enumerate(miss_pos):
189	                ai_by_pos[pos] = ai_miss[local_i]
190	        for i in range(n):  # reconstruct cached hits as AI-like objects
191	            if hits[i] is not None:
192	                ai_by_pos[i] = _CachedAI(hits[i].get("generation", ""), hits[i].get("tool_calls"))
193	
194	        prog(0.92, "Computing usage stats...")
195	        generations: list[dict] = []
196	        tokens_in_total = tokens_out_total = success_count = cache_hit_count = 0
197	        ttl = settings.llm_completion_cache_ttl_s
198	        for idx, (item_msgs, ai) in enumerate(zip(msgs_list, ai_by_pos)):
199	            cached = hits[idx] is not None
200	            try:
201	                tokens_in = sum(model_obj.get_num_tokens(m.get("content") or "")
202	                                for m in item_msgs)
203	            except Exception:
204	                tokens_in = 0
205	            generation = getattr(ai, "content", "") if ai is not None else ""
206	            tool_calls = None
207	            if ai is not None:
208	                raw_calls = (getattr(ai, "additional_kwargs", None) or {}).get("tool_calls")
209	                if raw_calls:
210	                    tool_calls = raw_calls
211	            try:
212	                tokens_out = model_obj.get_num_tokens(generation) if generation else 0
213	            except Exception:
214	                tokens_out = 0
215	            # A tool-call-only response (empty content) is NOT a failure.
216	            is_failure = not (bool(generation) and generation != "{}") and not tool_calls
217	            if not is_failure:
218	                success_count += 1
219	            if cached:
220	                cache_hit_count += 1
221	            elif rc and not is_failure:  # store fresh successes
222	                try:
223	                    rc.setex(keys[idx], ttl, json.dumps(
224	                        {"generation": generation, "tool_calls": tool_calls}, ensure_ascii=False))
225	                except Exception as exc:
226	                    logger.warning("llm.cache.write_failed", error=str(exc)[:120])
227	            generations.append({
228	                "prompt_id": idx, "generation": generation, "tool_calls": tool_calls,
229	                "error": "model_returned_empty" if is_failure else None,
230	                "cached": cached, "tokens_in": tokens_in, "tokens_out": tokens_out,
231	            })
232	            if not cached:  # totals reflect only the fresh LLM calls
233	                tokens_in_total += tokens_in
234	                tokens_out_total += tokens_out
235	
236	        input_price = getattr(model_obj, "input_price", 0.0) or 0.0
237	        output_price = getattr(model_obj, "output_price", 0.0) or 0.0
238	        currency = getattr(model_obj, "currency_code", "USD") or "USD"
239	        cost = input_price * tokens_in_total + output_price * tokens_out_total
240	        prog(1.0, "Completed")
241	        return {
242	            "generations": generations,
243	            "summary": {
244	                "model": resolved_model, "total": len(prompts), "success": success_count,
245	                "error_count": len(prompts) - success_count,
246	                "cache_hits": cache_hit_count,
247	                "tokens_in_total": tokens_in_total, "tokens_out_total": tokens_out_total,
248	                "cost": cost, "currency_code": currency,
249	                "elapsed_s": round(time.time() - start, 2),
250	            },
251	        }

253	    async def submit_batch(
254	        self,
255	        request: LLMBatchRequest,
256	        client_id: str = "",
257	    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
258	        """Validate the request, submit run_llm_batch, return the async envelope.
259	
260	        Returns the standard async response envelope with task_id, queue_load,
261	        and a polling hint pointing at /api/v1/tasks/{task_id}.
262	        """
263	        n = len(request.prompts)
264	        cap = settings.llm_batch_max_prompts
265	        if n > cap:
266	            raise ValidationError(
267	                f"Too many prompts: {n} > {cap}. Use llm_labeling on a datasource for larger workloads.",
268	                details={"limit": cap, "received": n},
269	            )
270	
271	        # Model resolution at submit time — bad models become 400, not 500.
272	        resolved_model, _model_obj = _resolve_model_or_raise(request.model)
273	
274	        from mxlens.services.queue_load import get_queue_load_snapshot
275	        from mxlens.services.task_service import TaskService
276	        from mxlens.workers.llm_tasks import run_llm_batch
277	
278	        # Tools and tool_choice are top-level on the request, but on the wire
279	        # we fold them into `config` because that's what the worker forwards
280	        # as **call_overrides to LiteLLM. Keeping a single fan-in point means
281	        # there's no second forwarding layer to keep in sync.
282	        merged_config: dict = dict(request.config) if request.config else {}
283	        if request.tools is not None:
284	            merged_config["tools"] = request.tools
285	        if request.tool_choice is not None:
286	            merged_config["tool_choice"] = request.tool_choice
287	
288	        task = run_llm_batch.apply_async(
289	            args=[_serialize_prompts(request.prompts)],
290	            kwargs={
291	                "model": resolved_model,
292	                "config": merged_config,
293	                "client_id": client_id,
294	            },
295	            queue="llm",
296	        )
297	
298	        await TaskService().register_task(task.id, client_id, "llm_batch")
299	        logger.info(
300	            "llm_batch.submitted",
301	            task_id=task.id,
302	            client_id=client_id,
303	            model=resolved_model,
304	            prompts=n,
305	        )
306	
307	        snapshot = await get_queue_load_snapshot(queue="llm")
308	        return {
309	            "task_id": task.id,
310	            "status": "pending",
311	            "operation": "llm_batch",
312	            "queue_load": snapshot,
313	            "next": {
314	                "polling_url": f"/api/v1/tasks/{task.id}",
315	                "result_url": f"/api/v1/tasks/{task.id}/result",
316	            },
317	        }
318	
319	    def estimate(self, request: LLMBatchEstimateRequest) -> LLMBatchEstimateResponse:
320	        """Synchronous token / cost estimate. No LLM call.
321	
322	        Counts input tokens by tokenizing each rendered prompt with the
323	        model's own tokenizer. Output tokens are estimated from a fixed
324	        per-prompt assumption (server config or per-request override).
325	        """
326	        n = len(request.prompts)
327	        cap = settings.llm_batch_max_prompts
328	        if n > cap:
329	            raise ValidationError(
330	                f"Too many prompts: {n} > {cap}. Use llm_labeling on a datasource for larger workloads.",
331	                details={"limit": cap, "received": n},
332	            )
333	
334	        resolved_model, model_obj = _resolve_model_or_raise(request.model)
335	        output_per_prompt = (
336	            request.output_tokens_per_prompt
337	            if request.output_tokens_per_prompt is not None
338	            else settings.llm_batch_estimate_output_tokens
339	        )
340	
341	        input_token_total = 0
342	        for p in request.prompts:
343	            if isinstance(p, str):
344	                input_token_total += _safe_token_count(model_obj, p)
345	            else:
346	                for m in p:
347	                    # content may be None on tool-calling assistant turns;
348	                    # _safe_token_count tolerates None.
349	                    input_token_total += _safe_token_count(model_obj, m.content)
350	                    # tool_calls / tool_call_id / name also consume input
351	                    # tokens via the serialized JSON. Tokenize the
352	                    # serialized blob so the estimate stays meaningful for
353	                    # multi-turn tool-calling conversations.
354	                    if m.tool_calls:
355	                        import json
356	                        input_token_total += _safe_token_count(
357	                            model_obj, json.dumps(m.tool_calls, ensure_ascii=False)
358	                        )
359	
360	        # Tools (function definitions) are sent once per call and contribute
361	        # to every prompt's input tokens. Tokenize the JSON blob and
362	        # multiply by the number of calls.
363	        if request.tools:
364	            import json
365	            tools_tokens = _safe_token_count(
366	                model_obj, json.dumps(request.tools, ensure_ascii=False)
367	            )
368	            input_token_total += tools_tokens * n
369	
370	        input_price = getattr(model_obj, "input_price", 0.0) or 0.0
371	        output_price = getattr(model_obj, "output_price", 0.0) or 0.0
372	        currency = getattr(model_obj, "currency_code", "USD") or "USD"
373	        output_token_total = output_per_prompt * n
374	        cost = input_price * input_token_total + output_price * output_token_total
375	
376	        return LLMBatchEstimateResponse(
377	            model=resolved_model,
378	            num_calls=n,
379	            input_token_total=input_token_total,
380	            output_token_total_assumed=output_token_total,
381	            input_price_per_token=float(input_price),
382	            output_price_per_token=float(output_price),
383	            estimated_cost=float(cost),
384	            currency_code=currency,
385	        )
386	
387	
388	def _safe_token_count(model_obj, text: str) -> int:
389	    try:
390	        return int(model_obj.get_num_tokens(text or ""))
391	    except Exception:
392	        # Fallback: rough character-based estimate, matching the one inside
393	        # LiteLLM2Chat.get_num_tokens for tokenizer failures.
394	        return max(1, len(text or "") // 2)
395	
```

1	"""Pydantic schemas for the generic LLM batch endpoint.
2	
3	This endpoint is intentionally minimal: caller hands in fully-rendered prompts,
4	endpoint dispatches one LLM call per prompt and returns raw generations. Row-
5	packing batch labeling lives in `llm_labeling` (datasource-backed); this is the
6	"arbitrary LLM call" escape hatch.
7	
8	The schema also supports OpenAI/LiteLLM-style function (tool) calling:
9	- Pass `tools=[...]` and optional `tool_choice` on the request.
10	- Use `role="tool"` messages (with `tool_call_id`) to return tool results in
11	  multi-turn React-style loops.
12	- Assistant turns that called tools may have empty `content` together with a
13	  `tool_calls` list.
14	"""
15	
16	from typing import Literal
17	
18	from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, model_validator
19	
20	
21	class ChatMessage(BaseModel):
22	    """One turn of a chat conversation, including tool-calling support.
23	
24	    For plain user / system / assistant turns, set `role` and `content`.
25	
26	    For OpenAI/LiteLLM-style tool calling:
27	      - An assistant turn that called tools may have empty `content` and a
28	        non-empty `tool_calls` list (each item is the OpenAI wire shape:
29	        ``{id, type: 'function', function: {name, arguments}}``).
30	      - A tool result is sent back as ``role="tool"`` with `tool_call_id`
31	        identifying the call it answers, plus `content` (the tool output)
32	        and optional `name`.
33	    """
34	
35	    role: Literal["system", "user", "assistant", "tool"]
36	    content: str | None = Field(
37	        default=None,
38	        description="Message text. May be empty/None on assistant turns that only carry tool_calls.",
39	    )
40	    # Assistant-only — the tool calls the model wants the caller to execute.
41	    # Permissive shape (list[dict]) because the wire format is stable and
42	    # callers typically echo back what they previously received.
43	    tool_calls: list[dict] | None = Field(
44	        default=None,
45	        description=(
46	            "Assistant-only. OpenAI/LiteLLM tool-call wire shape: a list of "
47	            '{id, type: "function", function: {name, arguments}} dicts. '
48	            "`arguments` is a JSON string per the OpenAI contract."
49	        ),
50	    )
51	    # Tool-only — identifies which tool call this message is answering.
52	    tool_call_id: str | None = Field(
53	        default=None,
54	        description="Required on role='tool': the id of the assistant tool_call this message answers.",
55	    )
56	    name: str | None = Field(
57	        default=None,
58	        description="Optional tool name (used on role='tool' to disambiguate).",
59	    )
60	
61	    @model_validator(mode="after")
62	    def _validate_per_role(self):
63	        if self.role == "tool":
64	            if not self.tool_call_id:
65	                raise ValueError("role='tool' requires tool_call_id")
66	            if self.content is None:
67	                raise ValueError("role='tool' requires content (the tool output)")
68	        elif self.role == "assistant":
69	            if not self.content and not self.tool_calls:
70	                raise ValueError("role='assistant' requires content or tool_calls")
71	        else:  # system, user
72	            if not self.content:
73	                raise ValueError(f"role='{self.role}' requires non-empty content")
74	        return self
75	
76	
77	# A single prompt is either a bare string (treated as a user-turn) or a full
78	# message list. The polymorphic shape lets simple callers stay simple while
79	# advanced callers retain full control over system prompts, multi-turn
80	# conversations, and tool calling.
81	PromptItem = str | list[ChatMessage]
82	
83	
84	class LLMBatchRequest(BaseModel):
85	    """Submit a batch of fully-rendered prompts for asynchronous LLM execution."""
86	
87	    prompts: list[PromptItem] = Field(
88	        ...,
89	        min_length=1,
90	        description=(
91	            "Fully-rendered prompts. Each item is either a string (single user "
92	            "message) or a list of {role, content, ...} turns. Item count is "
93	            "capped by settings.llm_batch_max_prompts."
94	        ),
95	    )
96	    model: str | None = Field(
97	        default=None,
98	        description=(
99	            "Model id known to mxapi.chatmodels.menu (e.g. 'doubao-1.8', "
100	            "'deepseek-v4-lite'). Defaults to settings.llm_default_model."
101	        ),
102	    )
103	    config: dict | None = Field(
104	        default=None,
105	        description=(
106	            "LiteLLM completion overrides forwarded per call (temperature, "
107	            "top_p, max_tokens, async_limit, ...). Keys unknown to the model "
108	            "are passed through to litellm."
109	        ),
110	    )
111	    tools: list[dict] | None = Field(
112	        default=None,
113	        description=(
114	            "OpenAI/LiteLLM function-calling tool definitions. Each item is "
115	            '{type: "function", function: {name, description, parameters}}. '
116	            "Forwarded verbatim to every call in the batch."
117	        ),
118	    )
119	    tool_choice: str | dict | None = Field(
120	        default=None,
121	        description=(
122	            "Tool selection: 'auto' | 'none' | 'required' | "
123	            '{type: "function", function: {name}}. Forwarded verbatim.'
124	        ),
125	    )
126	
127	
128	class LLMBatchEstimateRequest(BaseModel):
129	    """Synchronous pre-flight token / cost estimate for a batch."""
130	
131	    prompts: list[PromptItem] = Field(..., min_length=1)
132	    model: str | None = None
133	    # Allow caller to override the assumed output-token count for the
134	    # estimate; defaults to settings.llm_batch_estimate_output_tokens.
135	    output_tokens_per_prompt: int | None = Field(
136	        default=None,
137	        ge=0,
138	        description="Assumed completion tokens per prompt (defaults to server config).",
139	    )
140	    # Tools contribute non-trivially to input tokens; include them when present
141	    # so the estimate stays meaningful for tool-calling batches.
142	    tools: list[dict] | None = Field(
143	        default=None,
144	        description="Tool definitions (same shape as LLMBatchRequest.tools). Counted toward input tokens.",
145	    )
146	
147	
148	class LLMBatchEstimateResponse(BaseModel):
149	    """Result of `/llm/batch/estimate` — no LLM call made."""
150	
151	    model: str
152	    num_calls: int
153	    input_token_total: int
154	    output_token_total_assumed: int
155	    input_price_per_token: float
156	    output_price_per_token: float
157	    estimated_cost: float
158	    currency_code: str
159

2	from .menu import inner_menu, api_menus, _CLASS_MODULES

4	import importlib

7	
8	# Cache for lazily loaded classes
9	_class_cache = {}
10	
11	
12	def _resolve_class(cls_name: str):
13	    """Lazily import and return model class.
14	
15	    This allows using models without requiring all dependencies upfront.
16	    For example, using LiteLLM doesn't require boto3 (needed for BedRock).
17	    """
18	    if cls_name in _class_cache:
19	        return _class_cache[cls_name]
20	
21	    module_path = _CLASS_MODULES.get(cls_name)
22	    if not module_path:
23	        raise ValueError(f"Unknown model class: {cls_name}")
24	
25	    module = importlib.import_module(module_path, package='mxapi.chatmodels')
26	    cls = getattr(module, cls_name)
27	    _class_cache[cls_name] = cls
28	    return cls
29	
30	def check_modelId(modelId) -> str:
31	    if modelId in {None, "None", ""}:
32	        return None
33	    else:
34	        return modelId
35	
36	def model_illegal(modelId, format_type='api'):
37	    if format_type == "api":
38	        if modelId in api_menus:
39	            return api_menus
40	    elif format_type == 'inner':
41	        if modelId in inner_menu:
42	            return inner_menu
43	    else:
44	        return
45	
46	
47	def valid_model_ids(format_type='inner') -> set:
48	    """Return the set of registered model names for the given menu.
49	
50	    Single source of truth for "does this model exist" checks — reuse this
51	    instead of re-implementing menu membership at call sites.
52	    """
53	    return set(inner_menu if format_type == 'inner' else api_menus)
54	
55	
56	def list_models(format_type='inner') -> list:
57	    """Public, schema-stable view of registered chat models.
58	
59	    Returns one dict per model with only the caller-relevant fields; internal
60	    plumbing (``clsType``, raw endpoint id) is intentionally omitted. ``name``
61	    is the value to pass as ``llm_model``.
62	    """
63	    menu = inner_menu if format_type == 'inner' else api_menus
64	    return [
65	        {
66	            "name": name,
67	            "provider": m.get("provider", "litellm"),
68	            "max_token": m.get("max_token"),
69	            "output_max_token": m.get("output_max_token"),
70	            "input_price": m.get("input_price"),
71	            "output_price": m.get("output_price"),
72	            "currency_code": m.get("currency_code"),
73	        }
74	        for name, m in menu.items()
75	    ]
76	
77	def build_chat(modelId, 
78	               format_type='inner',
79	               **kwargs) -> [object, str]:
80	    
81	    modelId = check_modelId(modelId)
82	    if modelId is None:
83	        return None, f"ModelId:{modelId} illegal!"
84	
85	    ModelIdMenu = model_illegal(modelId, format_type)
86	
87	    if ModelIdMenu is None:
88	        return None, f"ModelId:{modelId} illegal!"
89	
90	    model_args= ModelIdMenu.get(modelId)
91	    logger.info(f'build_chat chat_model={modelId}, other args:{kwargs}')
92	
93	    # Build optional parameters dict
94	    optional_params = {}
95	    if 'output_max_token' in model_args:
96	        optional_params['output_max_token'] = model_args['output_max_token']
97	    if 'provider' in model_args:
98	        optional_params['provider'] = model_args['provider']
99	    if 'timeout' in model_args:
100	        optional_params['timeout'] = model_args['timeout']
101	    if 'async_limit' in model_args:
102	        optional_params['async_limit'] = model_args['async_limit']
103	    if 'llm_debug' in model_args:
104	        optional_params['llm_debug'] = model_args['llm_debug']
105	    if 'use_history' in model_args:
106	        optional_params['use_history'] = model_args['use_history']
107	    if 'completion_kwargs' in model_args:
108	        optional_params['completion_kwargs'] = model_args['completion_kwargs']
109	
110	    # Resolve string class name to actual class (lazy import)
111	    cls_type = model_args['clsType']
112	    if isinstance(cls_type, str):
113	        cls_type = _resolve_class(cls_type)
114	
115	    return cls_type(**{**kwargs,
116	                                    **{'modelId': model_args['id'],
117	                                       'max_token': model_args['max_token'],
118	                                       'input_price': model_args['input_price'],
119	                                       'output_price': model_args['output_price'],
120	                                       'currency_code': model_args['currency_code']
121	                                       },
122	                                    **optional_params
123	                                    }
124	                                 ), ''

=== fetch.py head (urllib pattern) ===
"""Download one source file into corpus/sources/<name>/ and record provenance."""
import hashlib, json, sys
from datetime import date
from pathlib import Path
from urllib.request import Request, urlopen


def fetch(url, dest_dir, license_note):
    dest = Path(dest_dir)
    dest.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    name = url.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
    req = Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": "scripture-loom-corpus/0.1"})
    data = urlopen(req).read()
    (dest / name).write_bytes(data)
    meta = {"url": url, "fetched": date.today().isoformat(),
            "license": license_note, "file": name,
            "sha256": hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()}
    (dest / "meta.json").write_text(json.dumps(meta, indent=1, sort_keys=True) + "\n")
    print(f"{name}: {len(data)} bytes sha256={meta['sha256'][:12]}…")
    return meta


if __name__ == "__main__":
    fetch(sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3])
```

# Content-Bank Authoring Pipeline — Investigation Report

## 1. The `content_bank` package and the modules issue #16 names

Package root: `/media/pb/data/pjllc/scripture_loom/content_bank/`. Layout: `author/` (prompt builders + store writer + manifest), `lib/` (corpus bridge, schema, validation, content store I/O), `store/` (committed book stores `mat.json`, `php.json`, `ecc.json`), `tests/`.

Every module issue #16 lists exists and each `build()` returns a **plain prompt string** (via `"\n".join(parts)`), fully self-contained (passage text, commentary, cross-refs, confessional refs, and output-shape instructions baked in). None make any network/LLM call — they are pure prompt assemblers.

**`content_bank/author/build_brief_prompt.py`**
- `build(pericope_id, book="MAT") -&gt; str` (line 52). Stage-1 "distillation pack" — passage text + WCF ch.1 + commentary + cross-refs + confessional proof-texts + a `_SHAPE` instruction block asking for a ~250-word 5-part theological brief. Returns the prompt string. Also has `main(argv)` CLI (`python3 -m content_bank.author.build_brief_prompt PID --book MAT`, optional `--out`).

**`content_bank/author/build_draft_prompt.py`**
- `build(pericope_id, book="MAT", brief=None) -&gt; str` (line 65). Stage-2 drafting pack. If `brief is None`, loads `content_bank/author/briefs/&lt;pid&gt;.lower().md` via `_load_brief` (raises `FileNotFoundError` if missing). Injects the brief, a compact WCF-1 guardrail, dimension templates, hard rules, allowed item types, rubric, and the output JSON schema block. Returns the prompt string. CLI `main` present (note: CLI path does not pass a brief through, always loads from disk).

**`content_bank/author/build_section_brief_prompt.py`**
- `build(section_id, book="MAT") -&gt; str` (line 31). Section-arc pack: all pericope texts in the section span + WCF ch.1 + `_SHAPE` asking for exactly one throughline, threads, and 2-4 arc questions. Returns prompt string. Imports `corpus.lib.sections`.

**`content_bank/author/store_writer.py`**
- `upsert_items(book, items, store_dir=None) -&gt; store dict` (line 11). The only committed code path that writes `store/&lt;book&gt;.json`. Replaces existing ids in place, appends new ones, writes pretty JSON, returns the full store dict. Stdlib `json` only.

**`content_bank/lib/corpus_bridge.py`**
- `passage_text(range_str, version="BSB") -&gt; str` (line 43). Resolves a range like `"MAT.5.3-12"` through `corpus/lib/passage.get_passage(...)`, returns verses joined as `"MAT.5.3  text\n..."` in canonical order. This is the verbatim-quote source the gate/agent check against. Sibling helpers: `pericopes(book)`, `book_name`, `wcf_chapter1_text`, `commentary`, `crossrefs`, `confessional_refs`.

**`content_bank/lib/schema.py`**
- `validate_item(item) -&gt; list[str]` (line 105). Structural validator; returns a list of error strings, empty == valid. Enforces required fields, controlled vocabularies (`DIMENSIONS` D1-D8, `TYPES`, `AGE_TIERS`, `REVIEW_STATUSES = {draft, reviewed, published}`), the passage-xor-section scope rule, `answer_key`↔closed-dims / `leader_note`↔open-dims, thread `refs` format `^[A-Z0-9]{3}\.\d+\.\d+$`, and provenance requirements once `review_status != "draft"` (needs `reviewed_by`, `reviewed_date`, `guardrail == "WCF-1"`). This is the schema gate.

## 2. The existing agent workflow (per pericope)

`work/content_bank_build/build_book_draft.workflow.js` is a Claude Code Workflow (`pipeline(pericopes, S1, S2)`), one agent per pericope, two phases:

- **S1 Brief:** agent runs `python3 -m content_bank.author.build_brief_prompt PID --book BOOK`, writes the brief markdown to `content_bank/author/briefs/&lt;pid&gt;.md`. (This is the first **LLM call** — turning the prompt pack into a brief.)
- **S2 Draft:** agent runs `build_draft_prompt`, drafts a JSON array of items to `work/content_bank_build/&lt;BOOK&gt;/drafts/&lt;pid&gt;.json`, then passes **two mechanical gates, repairing until clean**:
  1. **Quote gate:** `python3 work/content_bank_build/quote_check.py &lt;BOOK&gt; &lt;draft.json&gt;` — must print `{}`, exit 0.
  2. **Schema gate:** inline `schema.validate_item(i)` over every item — must print `OK`.
  Items carry `review_status "draft"`, `version 1`, `answer_key` (closed D1-D5) / `leader_note` (open D6-D8) with a provenance stub `{"reviewed_by":"claude-adversarial","reviewed_date":"2026-07-19","guardrail":"WCF-1"}`.

`build_sections_draft.workflow.js` is the section analog (one phase): runs `build_section_brief_prompt`, drafts exactly one `throughline` + zero-or-more `thread` (with `refs`) + 2-4 arc `question` items, same two gates.

**Gate scripts/functions:** the quote gate is `work/content_bank_build/quote_check.py` — `check(book, items) -&gt; dict` (line 60). It normalizes quote marks/whitespace, extracts quoted spans ≥ `MIN_WORDS = 3`, and flags any span not found verbatim in the **whole BSB** haystack (`corpus/canon/bibles/bsb.json`). Returns `{item_id: [missed spans]}`; `main` exits 1 if non-empty. The schema gate is `schema.validate_item`. Issue #16 also proposes a **new** `refs_in_range` gate (not yet implemented anywhere) to reject refs/quotes outside the pericope's own range.

**Staging as `review_status: reviewed`:** done by `work/content_bank_build/stage_book.py` (`main(argv)`, `_stage_item`). For each gated draft id it sets `review_status="reviewed"`, stamps provenance `{drafted_by:"claude", reviewed_by:"claude-adversarial", reviewed_date, guardrail:"WCF-1"}`, explicitly pops `confirmed_by`/`delegated_by` (nothing is human-confirmed), re-stamps each `leader_reference.provenance`, then `store_writer.upsert_items(book, staged)`, `manifest.set_stage(m, uid, "reviewed")`, and finally `validate.validate_store(book)`. Never sets `published` — that status is human-only.

## 3. The manifest (pending-unit tracking)

- Module: `content_bank/author/manifest.py`. `STAGES = ("pending","briefed","drafted","reviewed","in_queue","confirmed","published")`. Functions: `init_manifest(book, pericope_ids, section_ids=())`, `load(path)`, `save(path, manifest)`, `set_stage(manifest, unit_id, stage)`, and **`units_at(manifest, stage)`** which returns sorted unit ids at a given stage — this is exactly the "pending pericopes" iterator a builder would call.
- Format: `{"book": "MAT", "units": {"MAT-001": {"kind":"pericope","stage":"reviewed"}, ...}}`. Sections appear as `{"kind":"section", ...}` with ids like `MAT-S1`.
- Runtime location (untracked `work/`): `work/content_bank_build/&lt;BOOK&gt;/manifest.json` (present for MAT, PHP, ECC). Drafts live beside it in `&lt;BOOK&gt;/drafts/*.json`; verdicts under `&lt;BOOK&gt;/verdicts/`.

## 4. Stdlib-only constraint and existing HTTP/LLM code

- **Confirmed no dependency manifest.** No `requirements*.txt`, `setup.py`, or `pyproject.toml` anywhere. `CLAUDE.md` line 7 states: "No dependency manifest — everything is **Python 3 standard library only**, no third-party packages."
- **Existing HTTP-client usage (stdlib `urllib`):** only in the corpus ingest tree, not the content bank:
  - `corpus/ingest/fetch.py:5` — `from urllib.request import Request, urlopen`
  - `corpus/ingest/fetch_commentary_sources.py:41-42` — `from urllib.error import URLError` / `from urllib.request import Request, urlopen`
  These are the precedent for the issue's recommended option (b): hit the Anthropic REST endpoint via `urllib` to preserve stdlib-only.
- **No existing LLM-call code or `llm()` seam anywhere.** No `anthropic`, `openai`, `api_key`, or `llm(` references in the codebase. The "LLM call" in today's pipeline is performed by the Claude Code agent itself (the workflow `.js`), not by any committed Python.

## 5. Where `build_cli.py` and the `llm()` seam would live

- `content_bank/author/build_cli.py` **does not exist yet** — this is a net-new file. It belongs in `content_bank/author/` alongside the existing modules: `build_brief_prompt.py`, `build_draft_prompt.py`, `build_section_brief_prompt.py`, `build_reference_prompt.py`, `store_writer.py`, `manifest.py`, `digest.py`, `publish.py`, `rubric.py`, `dimensions.py`, `review_checklist.py`. `content_bank/author/briefs/` holds the committed per-pericope brief markdown the draft stage consumes.
- **The `llm()` seam** is the natural injection point for the HTTP call: today the two "write the brief" / "draft the items" agent turns are the only non-deterministic steps. A single `llm(prompt: str) -&gt; str` function (calling the Anthropic REST endpoint via stdlib `urllib.request`, mirroring `corpus/ingest/fetch.py`) would sit in `build_cli.py` (or a small sibling like `content_bank/author/llm.py`), invoked at exactly two points — after `build_brief_prompt.build(...)` and after `build_draft_prompt.build(...)` — plus the repair loop that re-feeds `quote_check.check` + `schema.validate_item` errors.
- **Test pattern:** `content_bank/tests/` uses `unittest` (e.g. `test_author.py`, `test_store_writer.py`, `test_manifest.py`, `test_schema.py`, `test_corpus_bridge.py`). Each test file does `sys.path.insert(0, parents[2])` then imports from `content_bank...`, subclasses `unittest.TestCase`, and ends with `unittest.main()`. A `content_bank/tests/test_build_cli.py` mocking the `llm()` seam (no network) would follow this exact convention — satisfying the issue's "unit tests with the LLM seam mocked" acceptance criterion.

### Issue #16 summary (retrieved via `gh api`)
Title: "Standalone Python content-bank builder (replace agent workflow with an API-call program)." It maps each agent shell-out to its pure callable (the table matches the modules above), proposes `content_bank/author/build_cli.py` iterating `manifest` pending units → `build()` brief → **LLM** → `build()` draft → **LLM** → parse JSON → gates → repair loop (≤N, gate errors fed back) → stage `reviewed` via `store_writer.upsert_items`. Adds a recommended new `refs_in_range` gate (would have caught the MAT-035 Matthew-15-quote-in-Matthew-8 drift). Open questions it flags: (a) `anthropic` SDK vs (b) stdlib `urllib` REST call (recommended, preserves the invariant); and whether to cover sections too. Acceptance criteria include the single mockable `llm()` seam, rate-limit backoff, per-unit failure isolation (`GateError`/`RateLimitError` → log and continue), and network-free unit tests.</result>

### 🤖 Assistant · 2026-07-20T04:28:00.715Z

I now have a complete picture from both my direct reads and the two agents. Here's the crux:

**What the mxlens "LLM REST API service" actually is** — a cleanly separable 3-layer stack: `mxapi/chatmodels/` (provider abstraction; the *only active* path is `LiteLLM2Chat` → LiteLLM → **Volcengine/doubao**, i.e. Chinese models — the Claude entries are commented out and `claudeapi.py` is a legacy cookie-scraper), a thin `LLMService.run_batch_sync()` core, and one router `POST /api/v1/llm/batch` that's **async** (Celery task on a Redis `llm` queue → poll `/tasks/{id}`). Transporting it drags in `fastapi + litellm + langchain (+ celery/redis if async kept)`.

**The tension:** scripture_loom's central CLAUDE.md invariant is **Python-stdlib-only, no third-party packages**. So a transported LLM service can't live *inside* the stdlib-only `content_bank`/`corpus` core — it has to be a quarantined component with its own deps, and the core calls it over stdlib `urllib` (exactly issue #16's recommended option b, and `corpus/ingest/fetch.py` already establishes the urllib pattern).

That makes the *shape* of the transport a genuine architectural decision that's yours to make. Let me confirm three things before I write the plan.

### 🤖 Assistant · 2026-07-20T04:36:15.297Z

Confirmed the contract. The only LLM endpoint mxlens exposes is **async**: `POST /api/v1/llm/batch` returns a `task_id`, then you poll `GET /api/v1/tasks/{task_id}` until `status == "completed"` and fetch `GET /api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/result` — all behind `x-api-key`. There's no synchronous one-shot endpoint.

**My honest take: yes, this is the easy way, and I'd endorse it for a first step.** No code to copy, no LLM libraries to vendor, mxlens stays the single source of truth. But two things worth your eyes open:

1. **It's a client against a *live* mxlens.** Your content builds now depend on a running mxlens stack (FastAPI + Celery worker + Redis + Volcengine credentials) being up and reachable. If it's down or moves, builds stall. That's a real runtime coupling for a Bible-content tool — fine if mxlens is stable infra you control, worth noting if not.
2. **The call is a submit→poll→fetch loop, not one request.** Plus per-request `model: "deepseek-v4-flash"`, an `x-api-key`, and a configured `api_base`. Modest, but more than a single POST.

And here's the part that makes it *even* easier than your earlier plan: **a client is just HTTP + JSON, which `urllib` does in the standard library.** So if we go client-only, you don't actually need to drop stdlib-only or adopt uv at all — the earlier uv decision existed only to host the *transported* service, which we're no longer doing. `corpus/ingest/fetch.py` already shows the urllib pattern in this repo.

So two quick things before I write the plan:

- **Do you still want to adopt uv / drop stdlib-only?** If the client is a thin stdlib `urllib` call, the simplest path keeps the repo stdlib-only and skips the whole packaging change. (Adopt uv only if you want it for other reasons.)
- **Scope of this prep step:** just add the client seam — e.g. `content_bank/author/llm.py` with `llm(prompt) -> text` that submits to mxlens and polls — configured via env (`MXLENS_API_BASE`, `MXLENS_API_KEY`, model)? Or also wire it into a `build_cli.py` now (that's really the issue #16 work)?

120	    messages = [

126	        cache=settings.analyst_llm_cache_enabled)
127	    s = out["summary"]

### 🤖 Assistant · 2026-07-20T04:55:17.419Z

That check is decisive. Here's the honest verdict on "can we use the same sync call outside mxlens":

**At the code level: yes.** `run_batch_llm` / `run_sync_llm` / `LLMService.run_batch_sync` are pure, in-process, synchronous — no Celery, no Redis (cache degrades to off if Redis is absent), no REST server, no poll loop. And mxlens's `.env` already contains the exact credential that path needs: `ARK_API_KEY=fe5d****` (deepseek-v4-flash routes through Volcengine Ark, keyed by `ARK_API_KEY`), so `llm_configured()` would return `True` immediately.

**But "outside the project" means importing that code, and that's where it gets non-trivial.** `uv add`-ing mxlens as a whole package is impractical:

- Its `pyproject.toml` pulls **torch, faiss-cpu, sentence-transformers, umap-learn, duckdb, minio, pandas, mxdb, flexdatapro-client**, plus `keywordfilter2`/`boolexpr` that resolve **only from a private index** (`http://pypi.mxspider.top/simple`), plus a Cython-built vendored `simhash-py`. You do not want any of that in scripture_loom just to make LLM calls, and the private index may not even be reachable from here.

The saving grace is mxlens's **lazy imports**: the *runtime* import chain for the sync call is small — `analyst/llm.py` → `config.settings` + `llm_service.py` → `mxapi.chatmodels` (LiteLLM path) + `llm_schemas.py`. The actual libraries touched are just **`litellm`, `langchain-core`, `tiktoken`, `pydantic`, `pydantic-settings`, `structlog`** — all public and light. The heavy deps are declared but never imported on this path.

So there's a real fork, and it changes what "use mxlens directly" means:

**Option A — HTTP client to a *running* mxlens.** No code copied. But the only LLM endpoint is async, so you inherit the submit→poll→result loop, an `x-api-key`, and a hard runtime dependency on a live mxlens stack (FastAPI + Celery worker + Redis + Ark) being up whenever you build content.

**Option B — Vendor the minimal *sync* core.** Copy ~6 files (`mxapi/chatmodels/` LiteLLM path + `llm_service.py` + `analyst/llm.py` + a trimmed `config` + `llm_schemas.py` + `utils/errors.py`), declare only the light public deps via uv, and call `run_batch_llm(...)` in-process. **No running server, no poll loop, no async** — and it's the same sync call the analyst uses. The cost is that you *do* copy a slice of mxlens (a mild form of the "transport" you wanted to avoid) and own it going forward.

Net: the sync call is the nicer developer experience, but it's fundamentally an **in-process library** (Option B), not "call the live service" (Option A). You can't have the sync call *and* zero copied code — the sync path only exists as importable Python, and importing all of mxlens is the heavy/private-index problem above.

Which way do you want to go — A (thin HTTP client, live mxlens, async poll) or B (vendor the light sync core, in-process, no server)?

### 🧑 User · 2026-07-20T04:59:41.355Z

option B

The one thing scripture_loom has no code for today is the\n**LLM call** — every other step is already a pure function.\n\n**This task is the preparation step for that:** give scripture_loom a real, in-process\n`llm()` capability, reusing mxlens's LLM machinery rather than writing a provider client\nfrom scratch.\n\n**Decisions made with the owner during planning:**\n- **Approach = Option B (vendor the sync core).** Copy the minimal *synchronous*,\n  in-process LLM path out of mxlens — the same one the mxlens \"analyst\" uses\n  (`run_sync_llm` / `run_batch_llm` → `LLMService.run_batch_sync` →\n  `chatmodels.build_chat().batch_llm()`). **No** FastAPI, **no** Celery, **no** Redis,\n  **no** REST endpoint, **no** poll loop, **no** running mxlens server.\n- **Adopt `uv`** as the package manager. The repo's long-standing *stdlib-only*\n  invariant is **dropped** (this is why uv is being added).\n- **Default model = `deepseek-v4-flash`** (Volcengine via LiteLLM, keyed by\n  `ARK_API_KEY`) — already mxlens's `analyst_llm_model`.\n- **Config = reuse mxlens's `.env`** at\n  `/media/pb/data/testbed/mxlens_service/mxlens/.env` (it already contains\n  `ARK_API_KEY`).\n- Installing all of mxlens is **out** — its `pyproject.toml` drags torch/faiss/duckdb/\n  minio/mxdb + private-index packages (`http://pypi.mxspider.top`). We vendor only the\n  files on the sync import path and pin only their light public deps.\n\nIntended outcome: `from llm_core import run_sync_llm; run_sync_llm(\"\", \"…\")` returns a\nreal completion from deepseek-v4-flash, in-process, with `uv run`, and a thin\n`content_bank` seam wraps it for the future builder.\n\n## Source → destination map (files to vendor)\n\nVendor from `/media/pb/data/testbed/mxlens_service/mxlens/` into a **new top-level\npackage `llm_core/`** in scripture_loom (mirrors the flat `corpus/`, `content_bank/`,\n`prototype/` layout). Preserve mxlens's sub-structure where it helps:\n\n| Source (mxlens) | Destination (scripture_loom) | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `mxapi/chatmodels/__init__.py` | `llm_core/chatmodels/__init__.py` | `build_chat`, lazy class loader — as-is |\n| `mxapi/chatmodels/menu.py` | `llm_core/chatmodels/menu.py` | **Trim** `_CLASS_MODULES`/menus to `LiteLLM2Chat` + `Fake2Chat` rows (deepseek-*/doubao-* + the fake stub); drop Bedrock/OpenAI/native-Volcengine/claudeapi rows |\n| `mxapi/chatmodels/basemodel.py` | `llm_core/chatmodels/basemodel.py` | as-is (stdlib only) |\n| `mxapi/chatmodels/basechain.py` | `llm_core/chatmodels/basechain.py` | needs `langchain-core` + `langchain-classic`; **try to trim** the `ConversationChain`/`RunnableWithMessageHistory` history wiring since batch calls are stateless (would drop `langchain-classic`) |\n| `mxapi/chatmodels/LiteLLM.py` | `llm_core/chatmodels/LiteLLM.py` | the workhorse; `batch_llm`/`chat`; make the `langchain_community.chat_message_histories` import lazy/guarded so `langchain-community` is only needed if history is used |\n| `mxapi/chatmodels/_aio_runner.py` | `llm_core/chatmodels/_aio_runner.py` | stdlib asyncio/threading — as-is |\n| `mxapi/chatmodels/fakemodel.py` | `llm_core/chatmodels/fakemodel.py` | `Fake2Chat` — enables network-free tests |\n| `mxlens/services/llm_service.py` | `llm_core/service.py` | keep `LLMService.run_batch_sync` + `estimate` + helpers; **delete `submit_batch`** (Celery/task_service/queue_load path). Drop the Redis branch or leave it dormant (cache defaults off) |\n| `mxlens/services/analyst/llm.py` | `llm_core/sync.py` | `run_sync_llm`, `run_batch_llm`, `llm_configured`, `_build_overrides` — the public seam |\n| `mxlens/models/llm_schemas.py` | `llm_core/schemas.py` | pydantic request/response models |\n| `mxlens/utils/errors.py` | `llm_core/errors.py` | just `ValidationError` (+ whatever it needs) |\n| — (new) | `llm_core/config.py` | **new trimmed settings** (see below), replaces `mxlens.config.settings` |\n| — (new) | `llm_core/__init__.py` | re-export `run_sync_llm`, `run_batch_llm`, `llm_configured` |\n| — (new) | `llm_core/PROVENANCE.md` | record vendored-from-mxlens + source commit, mirroring `corpus/PROVENANCE.md` convention |\n\n**Rewrite the internal imports** in the vendored files: `mxlens.config` →\n`llm_core.config`, `mxlens.services.llm_service` → `llm_core.service`,\n`mxlens.models.llm_schemas` → `llm_core.schemas`, `mxlens.utils.errors` →\n`llm_core.errors`, `mxapi.chatmodels` → `llm_core.chatmodels`.\n\n## Trimmed config (`llm_core/config.py`) — and the `.env`-into-`os.environ` gotcha\n\n`run_batch_sync` and `sync.py` reference only these settings fields:\n`llm_default_model`, `analyst_llm_model`, `llm_api_key`, `llm_api_base`,\n`analyst_llm_cache_enabled`, `llm_completion_cache_ttl_s`, `llm_batch_max_prompts`,\n`llm_batch_estimate_output_tokens`. Build a small `pydantic-settings` `BaseSettings`\nwith just these (`extra=\"ignore\"`, defaults: `analyst_llm_model=\"deepseek-v4-flash\"`,\n`analyst_llm_cache_enabled=False` so no Redis is ever touched).\n\n**Critical:** `LiteLLM2Chat._configure_provider` reads `ARK_API_KEY`/`VOLCENGINE_API_KEY`\nstraight from `os.environ`, and mxlens's `.env` sets `ARK_API_KEY` but **not**\n`llm_api_key`/`llm_api_base`. A pydantic `Settings` object does **not** populate\n`os.environ`. So `llm_core/config.py` must, on import, **load the `.env` file into the\nreal process environment** — add `python-dotenv` and call\n`load_dotenv(os.environ.get(\"SCRIPTURE_LOOM_LLM_ENV\", \"/media/pb/data/testbed/mxlens_service/mxlens/.env\"))`\nonce. This makes both the trimmed settings and chatmodels' direct `os.environ` reads see\n`ARK_API_KEY`. (Path overridable via `SCRIPTURE_LOOM_LLM_ENV`.)\n\n## content_bank seam\n\nAdd `content_bank/author/llm.py`:\n\n```python\ndef llm(prompt: str, model: str | None = None) -> str:\n    \"\"\"The single LLM seam issue #16's build_cli.py will call. Rendered prompt in,\n    completion text out. Import is lazy so importing content_bank does not pull litellm.\"\"\"\n    from llm_core import run_sync_llm\n    return run_sync_llm(\"\", prompt, caller=\"content_bank\", model=model)\n```\n\nThis matches the issue's \"LLM access encapsulated behind a single `llm()` seam\n(swappable/mockable)\" acceptance criterion. **`build_cli.py` and the gates/repair loop\nstay out of scope** — they are the follow-up issue #16 work.\n\n## uv adoption\n\n- Create root `pyproject.toml`: `name=\"scripture-loom\"`, `requires-python=\">=3.11\"`\n  (the vendored files use `str | None` / `from __future__ import annotations`), packages\n  = `llm_core`, `content_bank`, `corpus`, `prototype`.\n- Runtime deps (pin conservatively): `litellm`, `langchain-core`, `structlog`,\n  `pydantic`, `pydantic-settings`, `python-dotenv` (+ `langchain-classic` and/or\n  `langchain-community` **only if** the basechain/LiteLLM trims above can't remove the\n  history wiring). `tiktoken` comes in transitively via litellm.\n- Dev group: `pytest` (or keep `unittest`; see verification).\n- `uv sync` to generate `uv.lock`.\n- Do **not** copy mxlens's `[tool.uv.index]` private index — all our deps are on PyPI.\n\n## Docs to update\n\n- **`CLAUDE.md`**: rewrite the \"Repository state\" paragraph and remove the *stdlib-only,\n  no third-party packages* invariant; document uv (`uv sync`, `uv run …`), the new\n  `llm_core/` package, and the `.env`/`ARK_API_KEY` requirement for LLM calls. Note that\n  `corpus/` and `content_bank/` remain dependency-light by nature but are no longer\n  *required* to be.\n- **`llm_core/PROVENANCE.md`**: source repo path + commit, list of vendored files, and\n  the trims applied.\n\n## Critical files\n\n- New: `llm_core/` (all files above), `content_bank/author/llm.py`, root\n  `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `llm_core/tests/`.\n- Edited: `CLAUDE.md`.\n- Reference (unchanged, the future consumer): `content_bank/author/build_brief_prompt.py`,\n  `build_draft_prompt.py`.\n\n## Verification (end-to-end)\n\n1. **Install:** `cd /media/pb/data/pjllc/scripture_loom && uv sync` → resolves with no\n   private-index / heavy-dep entries; `uv.lock` written.\n2. **Network-free unit test** (`llm_core/tests/test_sync.py`): select the vendored\n   `fake` model (or monkeypatch `chatmodels.build_chat`) and assert `run_batch_llm`\n   returns one generation per prompt and `llm_configured()` behaves. Run:\n   `uv run python -m unittest discover -s llm_core/tests` (matches repo's unittest style)\n   — **must pass with no network**.\n3. **Real smoke test** (the actual proof it works), with the mxlens `.env` present:\n   `uv run python -c \"from llm_core import run_sync_llm; print(run_sync_llm('', 'Reply with exactly: pong'))\"`\n   → prints a real deepseek-v4-flash completion. Confirms `.env`→`os.environ` loading,\n   `ARK_API_KEY`, LiteLLM→Volcengine routing, and the sync path all work.\n4. **Seam check on a real pericope:**\n   `uv run python -c \"from content_bank.author.build_brief_prompt import build; from content_bank.author.llm import llm; print(llm(build('MAT-001'))[:400])\"`\n   → returns a drafted brief, exercising the exact path issue #16 will automate.\n5. **Regression:** existing suites still run under uv —\n   `uv run python -m unittest discover -s corpus/tests` and\n   `cd prototype && uv run python -m unittest test_selector`.\n\n## Follow-ups (explicitly NOT this task)\n\n- issue #16's `content_bank/author/build_cli.py`: manifest iteration, brief→draft→gates\n  →repair loop, `refs_in_range` gate, rate-limit backoff, per-unit failure isolation,\n  staging `reviewed` via `store_writer.upsert_items`.\n"
}

3	# Lazy import mapping: class name -> module path

5	_CLASS_MODULES = {
6	    "BedRock2Chat": ".BedRock",
7	    "Fake2Chat": ".fakemodel",
8	    "OpenAi2Chat": ".OpenAi",
9	    "Volcengine2Chat": ".Volcengine",
10	    "LiteLLM2Chat": ".LiteLLM",
11	}

13	# Cache for lazily loaded classes (module-level for __getattr__ access)
14	_class_cache = {}
15	
16	
17	def __getattr__(name: str):
18	    """Enable lazy class loading for backward compatibility.
19	
20	    This allows code like `isinstance(obj, menu.Volcengine2Chat)` to work
21	    without requiring all dependencies to be installed upfront.
22	    """
23	    if name in _CLASS_MODULES:
24	        if name not in _class_cache:
25	            module = importlib.import_module(_CLASS_MODULES[name], package="mxapi.chatmodels")
26	            _class_cache[name] = getattr(module, name)
27	        return _class_cache[name]

"""Unified error definitions for the application."""

from typing import Any, Optional


class MxSieveError(Exception):
    """Base exception for all MxSieve errors."""

    status_code: int = 500
    error_code: str = "INTERNAL_ERROR"

    def __init__(
        self,
        message: str,
        details: Optional[dict[str, Any]] = None,
    ):
        self.message = message
        self.details = details or {}
        super().__init__(message)

    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Convert error to dictionary for API response."""
        return {
            "error": {
                "code": self.error_code,
                "message": self.message,
                "details": self.details,
            }
        }


class NotFoundError(MxSieveError):
    """Resource not found."""

    status_code = 404
    error_code = "NOT_FOUND"


class ValidationError(MxSieveError):
    """Request validation failed."""

    status_code = 400
    error_code = "VALIDATION_ERROR"


class AuthenticationError(MxSieveError):
    """Authentication failed."""

    status_code = 401
    error_code = "AUTHENTICATION_ERROR"


class AuthorizationError(MxSieveError):
    """Authorization failed - insufficient permissions."""

    status_code = 403
    error_code = "AUTHORIZATION_ERROR"


class RateLimitError(MxSieveError):
    """Rate limit exceeded."""

    status_code = 429
    error_code = "RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED"

    def __init__(
        self,
        message: str = "Rate limit exceeded",
        retry_after: int = 60,
        details: Optional[dict[str, Any]] = None,
    ):
        self.retry_after = retry_after
        super().__init__(message, details)


class TaskError(MxSieveError):
    """Task-related error."""

    status_code = 500
    error_code = "TASK_ERROR"


class TaskNotFoundError(NotFoundError):
    """Task not found."""

    error_code = "TASK_NOT_FOUND"


class TaskCancelledError(TaskError):
    """Task was cancelled."""

    status_code = 400
    error_code = "TASK_CANCELLED"


class TaskFailedError(TaskError):
    """Task execution failed."""

    error_code = "TASK_FAILED"


class ExternalServiceError(MxSieveError):
    """External service call failed."""

    status_code = 502
    error_code = "EXTERNAL_SERVICE_ERROR"


class LLMError(ExternalServiceError):
    """LLM API call failed."""

    error_code = "LLM_ERROR"


class LogsUnavailableError(MxSieveError):
    """Log access requested but no log file is configured (LOG_FILE unset)."""

    status_code = 503
    error_code = "LOGS_UNAVAILABLE"


class DuplicateExecutionSuppressedError(MxSieveError):
    """A worker picked up a task_id that another worker is already running.

    Raised by ProgressTrackingTask.before_start when the Redis lease on
    task_running:{task_id} is held by another live worker (broker
    redelivery — visibility timeout or connection loss). The task ends
    in FAILURE on this worker; task_service.get_status cross-checks the
    lease and continues to report the task as RUNNING so callers keep
    polling the live instance.
    """

    status_code = 409
    error_code = "DUPLICATE_EXECUTION_SUPPRESSED"
```

21	    """
22	    Configure LiteLLM debug/logging settings.

62	def _extract_tool_calls(message) -> list:
63	    """Normalize LiteLLM's ``message.tool_calls`` into plain wire-format dicts.
64	
65	    LiteLLM returns ``tool_calls`` as objects with ``.id``, ``.type``, and
66	    ``.function.name`` / ``.function.arguments``. Convert to JSON-friendly
67	    dicts so the worker can return them as part of its result envelope
68	    without dragging LiteLLM types across the Celery boundary.
69	    """
70	    raw = getattr(message, "tool_calls", None) or []
71	    out: list = []
72	    for tc in raw:
73	        if isinstance(tc, dict):
74	            out.append(tc)
75	            continue
76	        try:
77	            out.append({
78	                "id": getattr(tc, "id", None),
79	                "type": getattr(tc, "type", "function"),
80	                "function": {
81	                    "name": tc.function.name,
82	                    "arguments": tc.function.arguments,
83	                },
84	            })
85	        except AttributeError:
86	            # Unknown shape — log and drop rather than fail the whole batch.
87	            logger.warning(f"[_extract_tool_calls] could not normalize: {tc!r}")
88	    return out
89

258	        """Validate the request, submit run_llm_batch, return the async envelope.

261	        and a polling hint pointing at /api/v1/tasks/{task_id}.
262	        """

265	        if n > cap:
266	            raise ValidationError(

268	                details={"limit": cap, "received": n},

279	        # we fold them into `config` because that's what the worker forwards

A pydantic\n``BaseSettings`` object does NOT populate ``os.environ`` — so we explicitly load\nthe ``.env`` file into the real process environment at import time. By default we\nreuse mxlens's ``.env`` (it already holds ``ARK_API_KEY``); override the path\nwith ``SCRIPTURE_LOOM_LLM_ENV``.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\n\nfrom dotenv import load_dotenv\nfrom pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict\n\n_DEFAULT_ENV = \"/media/pb/data/testbed/mxlens_service/mxlens/.env\"\n_ENV_PATH = os.environ.get(\"SCRIPTURE_LOOM_LLM_ENV\", _DEFAULT_ENV)\n\n# Load provider credentials into the real environment (override=False so an\n# already-exported ARK_API_KEY wins).   "content": "\"\"\"Network-free tests for the llm_core sync seam.\n\nThe LLM boundary (`LLMService.run_batch_sync`) is mocked, so no provider key,\nno litellm, and no network are exercised — this validates only our vendored\nglue: the config gate, prompt->messages mapping, and generation extraction.\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport unittest\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom unittest import mock\n\nsys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]))\n\nfrom llm_core import sync  # noqa: E402\n\n\nclass RunSyncLLMTest(unittest.TestCase):\n    def setUp(self):\n        # Gate passes without depending on the real .env being present.\n        os.environ.setdefault(\"ARK_API_KEY\", \"test-key\")\n\n    def test_maps_generation_text(self):\n        fake = {\"generations\": [{\"generation\": \"hello world\", \"error\": None}],\n                \"summary\": {\"tokens_out_total\": 2, \"cost\": 0.0}}\n        with mock.patch(\"llm_core.service.LLMService.run_batch_sync\",\n                        return_value=fake) as m, \\\n                mock.patch.object(sync, \"llm_configured\", return_value=True):\n            out = sync.run_sync_llm(\"sys\", \"user\", model=\"deepseek-v4-flash\")\n        self.assertEqual(out, \"hello world\")\n        self.assertEqual(m.call_count, 1)\n        # One prompt (list of messages) submitted, system+user turns.\n        (prompts,), kwargs = m.call_args\n        self.assertEqual(kwargs[\"model\"], \"deepseek-v4-flash\")\n        self.assertEqual(prompts[0][0], {\"role\": \"system\", \"content\": \"sys\"})\n        self.assertEqual(prompts[0][1], {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"user\"})\n\n    def test_batch_maps_and_fails_open_per_item(self):\n        fake = {\"generations\": [\n                    {\"generation\": \"a\", \"error\": None},\n                    {\"generation\": \"\", \"error\": \"model_returned_empty\"}],\n                \"summary\": {}}\n        with mock.patch(\"llm_core.service.LLMService.run_batch_sync\",\n                        return_value=fake), \\\n                mock.patch.object(sync, \"llm_configured\", return_value=True):\n            out = sync.run_batch_llm([(\"s1\", \"u1\"), (\"s2\", \"u2\")])\n        self.assertEqual(out, [\"a\", \"\"])\n\n    def test_unconfigured_raises(self):\n        with mock.patch.object(sync, \"llm_configured\", return_value=False):\n            with self.assertRaises(RuntimeError):\n                sync.run_sync_llm(\"\", \"hi\")\n\n    def test_empty_batch_short_circuits(self):\n        # No gate check, no LLM call for an empty batch.\n        self.assertEqual(sync.run_batch_llm([]), [])\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    unittest.main()\n"
}

### 🤖 Assistant · 2026-07-20T05:14:34.568Z

Done. The LLM service is transported and working end-to-end.

## What I built

Per your decisions (Option B, uv, `deepseek-v4-flash`, mxlens `.env`), I vendored the **minimal synchronous in-process LLM core** out of mxlens into a new `llm_core/` package — no FastAPI, no Celery, no Redis, no REST server, no poll loop.

**New package `llm_core/`** (vendored + rewired):
- `chatmodels/` — the LiteLLM provider path (`build_chat` → `batch_llm`), menu trimmed to LiteLLM/Fake classes
- `service.py` — `LLMService.run_batch_sync` + `estimate` (dropped the async Celery `submit_batch` and the Redis cache)
- `sync.py` — the public seam `run_sync_llm` / `run_batch_llm` / `llm_configured`
- `schemas.py`, `errors.py`, and a new trimmed `config.py`
- `PROVENANCE.md` recording the source commit (`dc980f0`) and every modification

**Wiring:**
- `content_bank/author/llm.py` — the `llm(prompt) -> text` seam issue #16's `build_cli.py` will call (lazy import, so importing `content_bank` doesn't pull litellm)
- Root `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` (uv, non-packaged); `CLAUDE.md` rewritten to drop the stdlib-only invariant; `.gitignore` gets `.venv/`

**The one real gotcha handled:** `.env` is loaded into `os.environ` at config import (not just into a Settings object), because `LiteLLM2Chat` reads `ARK_API_KEY` straight from the environment. Also guarded a stray `celery.exceptions` import in `batch_llm`.

## Verification (all green)
- `uv sync` — 76 packages, no torch/faiss/duckdb, no private index
- **Network-free unit tests** (LLM seam mocked): llm_core 4/4, content_bank seam 1/1
- **Real smoke test**: `run_sync_llm('', 'Reply with one word: pong')` → `'pong'` via deepseek-v4-flash
- **Real authoring path**: `build_brief_prompt.build('MAT-001')` → `llm()` → a 2352-char theological brief on Matthew 1:1-17
- **Regressions**: corpus 67, content_bank 129, prototype 34 — all pass under uv

## Notes
- Work is on branch `feature/llm-core-vendor`, **not committed** (you didn't ask me to). Say the word and I'll commit.
- Out of scope, as planned: issue #16's `build_cli.py` (manifest iteration, gates + `refs_in_range`, repair loop, staging) — that's the follow-up now that the `llm()` seam exists.

One thing worth flagging: `llm_core/config.py` defaults the `.env` path to the **absolute mxlens path** (`/media/pb/data/testbed/…`). That's convenient now but brittle if either repo moves. Want me to switch it to a repo-local `.env` (copying just the `ARK_API_KEY`) instead?
